Like, the other day, I was just standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I was just repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually going and closing out the checks. I don't really know why I was doing it, but it really freaked my manager out, so... that's why they wanted me to come see you.\n\nDoctor: When you were standing at the soda station repeating the table numbers, were you aware of what was happening around you at that moment, or did it feel like you were in some kind of fog or daze?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I don't think it felt like a fog, exactly. It's just... I don't really remember noticing what was going on around me. I was just focused on saying the numbers. I guess I don't really know how aware I was of the rest of the restaurant right then.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you're repeating the numbers or freezing up, do you completely lose consciousness, or are you aware of what's happening even if you can't control it?\n\nPatient: I definitely don't lose consciousness. Like, I don't pass out or fall down or anything like that. I'm awake. But... I don't know if I'd say I'm completely aware of everything, either. \n\nIt's more like my brain is just... stuck on whatever I'm doing right then, like saying the numbers. So I'm awake, and my eyes are open, but I'm just not really taking in anything else around me. I don't really know how to explain it better than that.\n\nDoctor: How often are these episodes happening? Are we talking about once a week, multiple times a week, or something else?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't been keeping track of exactly how often it happens. I know it's been enough times recently that my manager got really concerned and told me to come here, but... I couldn't tell you if it's exactly once a week or multiple times a week. I just don't really have a specific number for you.\n\nDoctor: After these episodes end, can you remember everything that happened while you were repeating the numbers or freezing up, or is there a blank spot in your memory for what occurred during that time?\n\nPatient: Um... I don't think it's a complete blank spot. Like, I know that I was doing it. I remember saying the numbers. But since I wasn't really noticing anything else while it was happening, I don't have any memory of what was going on around me in the restaurant. So... I guess I remember being stuck, but I just couldn't tell you what else happened during that time.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes happen where you freeze up or get stuck repeating the numbers, can you think of anything that triggered them? Like, was there something stressful happening at work right before it started, or did it just happen out of the blue?\n\nPatient: I've tried to think about that, actually. Um... I don't really think there's a specific trigger. Like, work is always a little busy since it's a restaurant, but it's not like I was having a really stressful moment or dealing with a bad customer right before it happened. \n\nIt really just... it seems to happen out of the blue. I'll just be doing my normal routine, and then it starts. So, no, I haven't noticed anything specific that sets it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any major stressful events or trauma in your life — things like abuse, accidents, loss of someone close to you, or other really difficult experiences?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't been through anything like that. No bad accidents or abuse, and I haven't lost anyone close to me. My life has been pretty... normal, I guess. Just regular everyday stress, but definitely no major trauma or anything really difficult like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes when you're stuck or repeating the numbers, does it ever feel like a different part of you or a different person is taking over, or do you always feel like it's just you doing these things?\n\nPatient: Um, no, it doesn't feel like that. It's definitely just me. I don't feel like another person or a different part of me is taking over or anything. Like I said before, I know I'm the one saying the numbers and standing there... I just get stuck. But it's always just me doing it.\n\nDoctor: When you're repeating those table numbers, do you feel like you're choosing to say them, or does it feel like the words are just coming out of your mouth on their own without you deciding to say them?\n\nPatient: Um... no, it doesn't really feel like that. I mean, I'm definitely the one choosing to say them. It's not like my mouth is just moving on its own or anything. \n\nIt's more that my brain just gets completely stuck on those specific numbers, and... I just feel like I have to say them out loud. But I'm always the one doing it. I don't feel like the words are just spilling out without me deciding to speak.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks or months before these episodes started happening, have you noticed any changes in your mood, sleep, or anxiety levels, or has anything been weighing on your mind — even if it doesn't feel directly connected to what's happening at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed any changes like that. My mood has been pretty much the same, I think. I sleep okay, just regular amounts. And I don't feel any more anxious than usual. There hasn't really been anything heavy weighing on my mind or anything different going on before all this started. Everything was just... normal.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes when you're stuck repeating the numbers, if someone had grabbed your arm or called your name loudly, do you think you would have been able to snap out of it and respond to them, or would you have been completely unable to stop what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm actually not sure. Nobody has ever really tried to grab my arm or yell my name when it happens. Usually, people just kind of look at me or walk away. So... I don't really know if I would snap out of it or if I'd still be stuck. I just haven't had that happen to say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I don't feel like the words are just spilling out without me deciding to speak.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few weeks or months before these episodes started happening, have you noticed any changes in your mood, sleep, or anxiety levels, or has anything been weighing on your mind — even if it doesn't feel directly connected to what's happening at work?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I haven't really noticed any changes like that. My mood has been pretty much the same, I think. I sleep okay, just regular amounts. And I don't feel any more anxious than usual. There hasn't really been anything heavy weighing on my mind or anything different going on before all this started. Everything was just... normal.\n\nDoctor: During one of these episodes when you're stuck repeating the numbers, if someone had grabbed your arm or called your name loudly, do you think you would have been able to snap out of it and respond to them, or would you have been completely unable to stop what you were doing?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm actually not sure. Nobody has ever really tried to grab my arm or yell my name when it happens. Usually, people just kind of look at me or walk away. So... I don't really know if I would snap out of it or if I'd still be stuck. I just haven't had that happen to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: When you're repeating those table numbers, does your body do anything unusual at the same time — like your arms or hands moving in a way you're not controlling, or your face twitching, or anything like that?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not that I've noticed. I don't think my body does anything weird. My hands aren't moving on their own or anything like that, and my face isn't twitching. It's really just... the numbers. I'm just standing there saying the numbers. I haven't felt my body doing anything out of my control.\n\nDoctor: When you're repeating those table numbers, does it feel like something you're doing because you feel you have to, or does it feel more like your voice or mouth is acting on its own without your control?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't think it's really either of those. It doesn't feel like something I have to do, like a rule or anything. And my mouth isn't moving on its own... I know I'm the one talking. It's just... the numbers just come out. I'd end up standing by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I'd just be repeating the table numbers instead of actually closing out the checks. So, no, I'm not making a conscious choice to begin doing it, it just happens.\n\nDoctor: When you're standing there repeating the table numbers, do you feel like you could stop yourself and say something different right now if you really tried, or does it feel impossible to stop once it's started?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was just saying a second ago... when I get stuck like that, freezing up by the soda station with the cash drawer key, I don't really try to stop. I'm just frozen in it. Like I was just saying, I'm not really thinking about it or choosing to say them. \n\nI think that automatic part is exactly why it scared everyone at work so much.
